Appendix A
PBH Dominated Era

In this section, we provide the full derivation of all relevant quantities of the PBH era and provide the exact expression in terms of
g_{\star}(T)
and
g_{\star,s}(T)
.
For that we assume a monochromatic mass function centered at
\langle M_{\rm{PBH}}\rangle
given by Eq. (
250
), and we model the PBH-dominated era with the smooth scale factor of Eq. (
45
), for which
\eta_{\star}=\eta_{\rm eq}/(\sqrt{2}-1)
and the horizon-crossing values are

k_{\rm eq}\eta_{\rm eq}=4-2\sqrt{2},\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad k_{\rm eva}\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>\to 2,

(248)

in the deep matter-dominated regime
\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>/\eta_{\rm eq}\gg 1
.

A.1
PBH formation

In this work, we focus on the so-called ultra-light PBH window evaporating before the onset of Big-Bang Nucleosynthesis (BBN),
t_{\rm eva}\lesssim 1~\rm s
, which upon using Eq. (
11
) imply that they are lighter than about
M_{{\rm PBH},f}\lesssim 10^{9}~\rm g
. From inverting Eq. (
250
), they would have formed at the temperature

T_{f}\simeq 3.1\times 10^{11}~{\rm GeV}~\gamma_{\rm H}^{1/2}\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}(T_{f})}\right)^{1/4}\left(\frac{10^{9}~\rm g}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{1/2}.

(249)

The quantity
M_{{\rm PBH},f}
refers to the initial average PBH mass at formation

M_{{\rm PBH},f}\equiv\gamma_{\rm H}M_{{\rm H},f}=\gamma_{\rm H}\frac{4\pi M_{\rm pl}^{2}}{H_{\rm f}}=12\sqrt{\frac{10}{g_{\star}{(T_{f})}}}\gamma_{\rm H}\frac{M_{\rm pl}^{3}}{T_{f}^{2}},

(250)

where
M_{{\rm H},f}
is the mass within the horizon when the Hubble expansion rate is
H_{f}
and the temperature is
T_{f}
.
The horizon mass fraction falling into the PBH is
\gamma_{\rm H}=\mathcal{O}(1)
for superhorizon collapse, see Sec.
2.2
for more details.

The PBH formation temperature
T_{\rm f}
cannot be higher than the maximal temperature
T_{\rm max}
of the universe given by

T_{\rm max}\simeq\left(\frac{3M_{\rm pl}^{2}H_{\rm end}^{2}}{\pi^{2}g_{\star}/30}\right)^{1/4}\;,

(251)

where
H_{\rm end}
is the Hubble scale at the end of inflation.
The non-observation of primordial B modes in CMB anisotropies by BICEP/Keck
[
6
]
provides the most stringent upper bound on the tensor-to-scalar ratio

r\equiv\frac{\Delta^{2}_{t}(k_{\rm CMB})}{\Delta^{2}_{s}(k_{\rm CMB})}\lesssim 0.036,

(252)

where
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{R}}(k_{\rm CMB})\simeq 2.099\times 10^{-9}
is the curvature power spectrum measured by Planck at the CMB scale
k_{\rm CMB}=0.05~\rm Mpc^{-1}

[
10
]
. In the slow-roll approximation, the tensor power spectrum reads
[
46
]

\Delta_{t}^{2}(k)=\frac{2}{\pi^{2}}\frac{H_{k}^{2}}{M_{\rm pl}^{2}}\;.

(253)

Using Eq. (
253
), we can translate the BICEP/Keck bound in Eq. (
252
) to an upper bound on the maximal reheating temperature of the universe in Eq. (
251
)

T_{\rm max}\simeq 5.8\times 10^{15}~{\rm GeV}\left(\frac{r}{0.036}\right)^{1/4}\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}}\right)^{1/4}\left(\frac{H_{\rm end}}{H_{\rm CMB}}\right)^{1/2},

(254)

where
H_{\rm CMB}
is the Hubble rate when
k_{\rm CMB}
exits the horizon. The final ratio in Eq. (
254
) depends on the running of the tensor power spectrum
d\Delta^{2}_{t}(k)/d\ln(k)
. Assuming the power-law
\Delta_{t}^{2}(k)=A_{t}\left(k/k_{\rm CMB}\right)^{n_{t}}
, we obtain
\left({H_{\rm end}/H_{\rm CMB}}\right)^{\!1/2}=\left({k_{\rm end}}/{k_{\rm CMB}}\right)^{\!n_{t}/2}\leq 1
leading to
H_{\rm end}/H_{\rm CMB}\to 1
for
n_{t}\to 0
. Plugging Eqs. (
250
) and (
254
) into the condition
T_{f}<T_{\rm max}
leads to the following lower bound on the PBH mass

M_{{\rm PBH},f}>M_{\rm min}\simeq 2.8~{g}~\gamma_{\rm H}\left(\frac{0.036}{r}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{H_{\rm CMB}}{H_{\rm end}}\right),

(255)

where
1~{\rm g}\simeq 5.0\times 10^{-34}~M_{\odot}
. This explain the gray regions in Figs.
2
.

A.2
PBH domination

Using
H=1/2t
in Eq. (
11
), we obtain the temperature of the universe when PBHs evaporate

T_{\rm eva}=\left(\frac{405}{2\pi^{2}}\right)^{\!1/4}\frac{A^{1/2}M_{\rm pl}^{5/2}}{g_{\star}^{1/4}(T_{\rm eva})\,M_{{\rm PBH},f}^{3/2}}\simeq 0.76~{\rm MeV}\,\left(\frac{g_{{\rm H}\star}}{108}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}\right)^{1/4}\left(\frac{10^{9}~\rm g}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{3/2}.

(256)

where
g_{\star}
is the number of relativistic degrees of freedom and
A\equiv\pi\,\mathcal{G}\,g_{{\rm H}\star}/480\simeq 2.7
(see Eq. (
8
)). This would also be the reheating temperature of the universe if PBHs evaporate while dominating the universe.
The universe becomes PBH-dominated if the reheating temperature
T_{\rm eva}
is lower than the temperature
T_{\rm eq}
when PBHs start dominating the universe

T_{\rm eq}=\frac{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}{h_{\star}(T_{f})}\,T_{f}\,\beta_{f}=9.7~{\rm TeV}~\gamma_{\rm H}^{1/2}\left(\frac{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}{h_{\star}(T_{f})}\right)\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}(T_{f})}\right)^{\!1/4}\!\left(\frac{10^{8}~\rm g}{M_{\rm{PBH}}}\right)^{\!1/2}\!\left(\frac{\beta_{f}}{10^{-8}}\right).

(257)

where we have introduced

h_{\star}(T)\equiv\frac{g_{\star,s}(T)}{g_{\star}(T)},

(258)

which equals
h_{\star}(T)=1
if
g_{\star}(T)=g_{\star,s}(T)
.
PBHs dominate the energy density before they
evaporate if their initial abundance
\beta_{f}
, defined as the fraction of the total energy density at formation,

\beta_{f}\equiv\Omega_{\mathrm{PBH},\mathrm{f}}=\frac{\rho_{\mathrm{PBH},\mathrm{f}}}{3H_{\mathrm{f}}^{2}M_{\mathrm{pl}}^{2}}.

(259)

exceeds a critical threshold
\beta_{f}>\beta_{c}
.
Plugging Eqs. (
256
) and (
257
) into the condition for PBH domination
T_{\rm eq}>T_{\rm eva}
, we find

\beta_{\rm c}=\frac{h_{\star}(T_{f})}{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\frac{T_{\rm eva}}{T_{f}}=2.5\times 10^{-14}~\gamma_{\rm H}^{-1/2}\left(\frac{g_{\mathrm{H}\star}}{108}\right)^{\!1/2}\left(\frac{h_{\star}(T_{f})}{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\right)\left(\frac{g_{\star}(T_{f})}{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}\right)^{\!1/4}\!\left(\frac{10^{8}\,{\rm g}}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right).

(260)

From Eqs. (
257
) and (
260
), notice also the relationship

\beta_{c}~=~\beta_{f}\frac{T_{\rm eva}}{T_{\rm eq}}.

(261)

PBH evaporation following domination changes the comoving entropy density of the universe,
S\propto g_{\star,s}T^{3}a^{3}
, by a factor

D\equiv\frac{S(T_{\rm eva})}{S(T_{\rm eva}^{-})}=\frac{S(T_{\rm eva})}{S(T_{\rm eq})}=\frac{g_{\star,s}(T_{\rm eva})}{g_{\star,s}(T_{\rm eq})}\left(\frac{T_{\rm eva}\,a(T_{\rm eva})}{T_{\rm eq}\,a(T_{\rm eq})}\right)^{\!3},

(262)

where
T_{\rm eva}^{-}
and
T_{\rm eva}
denote the temperatures of the universe immediately before and after evaporation, respectively. In the second equality of Eq. (
262
), we have used conservation of the comoving entropy from
T_{\rm eq}
to
T_{\rm eva}^{-}
, that is
S(T_{\rm eva}^{-})=S(T_{\rm eq})
, while the third equality is the definition
S\propto g_{\star,s}T^{3}a^{3}
.
It can be useful to rewrite
D
as

D=\frac{g_{\star,s}(T_{\rm eva})}{g_{\star,s}(T_{\rm eq})}\left(\frac{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}\right)^{\!3/4}\left(\frac{a_{\rm eva}}{a_{\rm eq}}\right)^{\!3/4}=\frac{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\frac{T_{\rm eq}}{T_{\rm eva}}=\frac{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\frac{\beta_{f}}{\beta_{c}},

(263)

where
h_{\star}(T)
is defined in Eq. (
258
). In the first equality of Eq. (
263
), we have substituted the temperature ratio
T_{\rm eva}/T_{\rm eq}
that follows from
a^{3}(T_{\rm eva})/a^{3}(T_{\rm eq})=\rho_{\rm m}(T_{\rm eq})/\rho_{\rm m}(T_{\rm eva})
together with the radiation-matter equality conditions
\rho_{\rm m}(T_{\rm eq})=\rho_{\rm r}(T_{\rm eq})
and
\rho_{\rm m}(T_{\rm eva})=\rho_{\rm r}(T_{\rm eva})
. Using
\rho_{\rm r}=(\pi^{2}/30)\,g_{\star}T^{4}
, these conditions give
T_{\rm eva}/T_{\rm eq}=\left(g_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})/g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})\right)^{1/4}\left(a_{\rm eq}/a_{\rm eva}\right)^{3/4}
. The evaporation is assumed to occur instantaneously, i.e.
a(T_{\rm eva}^{-})\simeq a(T_{\rm eva})
. The second equality of Eq. (
263
) follows from inserting the same relation once more to trade
\left(a_{\rm eva}/a_{\rm eq}\right)^{3/4}
for
T_{\rm eq}/T_{\rm eva}
and regrouping the statistical weights into
h_{\star}=g_{\star,s}/g_{\star}
. In the last equality, we have used Eqs. (
257
) and (
260
) to express the dilution factor
D
in terms of the ratio of the PBH energy fraction at formation
\beta_{f}
divided by the threshold value
\beta_{c}
for generating a PBH-dominated era.
Using Eqs. (
260
), we can express
D
as a function of PBH parameters

D\simeq 4.1\times 10^{6}~\gamma_{\rm H}^{1/2}\left(\frac{108}{g_{\star\rm H}}\right)^{\!1/2}\left(\frac{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}{h_{\star}(T_{f})}\right)\left(\frac{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}{g_{\star}(T_{f})}\right)^{\!1/4}\!\left(\frac{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}{10^{9}~\rm g}\right)\left(\frac{\beta_{f}}{10^{-8}}\right).

(264)

PBH evaporation following a PBH-dominated era injects entropy
D>1
. The case of an adiabatic universe expansion, e.g. when PBHs never dominate the energy density of the universe, can be recovered for
D=1
.
In order to ensure that
D\geq 1
, we shift
D\to 1+D
in our numerical treatment.

A.3
Hubble expansion during matter era

From the last equality of Eq. (
263
),

\frac{\beta_{f}}{\beta_{c}}=\left(\frac{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\right)^{\!1/4}\left(\frac{a_{\rm eva}}{a_{\rm eq}}\right)^{\!3/4},

(265)

which requires the expansion factor
a_{\rm eva}/a_{\rm eq}
across the PBH-dominated era. Evaluating the first branch of the smooth scale factor Eq. (
45
) at
\eta=\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>
, in the deep matter-dominated regime
\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>/\eta_{\rm eq}\gg 1
,

\frac{a_{\rm eva}}{a_{\rm eq}}=\left(\frac{\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>}{\eta_{\star}}\right)^{\!2}+2\,\frac{\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>}{\eta_{\star}}\simeq\left(\frac{\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>}{\eta_{\star}}\right)^{\!2}.

(266)

The horizon-crossing definitions
k_{\rm eq}=(4-2\sqrt{2})/\eta_{\rm eq}
and
k_{\rm eva}\to 2/\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>
, with
\eta_{\star}=\eta_{\rm eq}/(\sqrt{2}-1)
, give

\frac{k_{\rm eq}}{k_{\rm eva}}=(2-\sqrt{2})\,\frac{\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>}{\eta_{\rm eq}}=\frac{2-\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{2}-1}\,\frac{\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>}{\eta_{\star}}=\sqrt{2}\,\frac{\left<\eta_{\rm eva}\right>}{\eta_{\star}},

(267)

using
\eta_{\rm eq}=(\sqrt{2}-1)\eta_{\star}
and
(2-\sqrt{2})/(\sqrt{2}-1)=\sqrt{2}
. Squaring Eq. (
267
) and inserting Eq. (
266
) gives the geometric link
19
19

19

Neglecting the order-unity matching at matter-radiation equality, i.e. extrapolating the deep matter-dominated relation
\mathcal{H}=2/\eta
down to
\eta_{\rm eq}
so that
k_{\rm eq}\eta_{\rm eq}\to 2
, gives the commonly quoted
a_{\rm eva}/a_{\rm eq}=(k_{\rm eq}/k_{\rm eva})^{2}
, hence
D=c_{2}(k_{\rm eq}/k_{\rm eva})^{3/2}
and
N_{\rm MD}=2\ln(k_{\rm eq}/k_{\rm eva})
as in Ref.
[
211
]
. Using instead the equality value
k_{\rm eq}\eta_{\rm eq}=4-2\sqrt{2}
of the smooth background Eq. (
45
) introduces the constant factor
1/2
in
a_{\rm eva}/a_{\rm eq}
, equivalently
2^{-3/4}\simeq 0.60
in
D
and a
-\ln 2
shift in
N_{\rm MD}
.

\frac{a_{\rm eva}}{a_{\rm eq}}\simeq\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{k_{\rm eq}}{k_{\rm eva}}\right)^{\!2}.

(268)

A.4
Comoving scales

An important scale is the comoving PBH interspacing above which the PBH gas description fails
[
293
]

k_{\rm{PBH}}\equiv a(T_{f})\left(\frac{4\pi}{3}\overline{n}_{\rm{PBH}}(t_{f})\right)^{1/3}=a(T_{f})\left(\frac{4\pi}{3}\frac{\beta_{f}\rho_{\rm r}(T_{f})}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{1/3}.

(269)

Using
a(T_{f})/a(T_{0})=(T_{0}/T_{f})(g_{\star,s}(T_{0})/g_{\star,s}(T_{f}))^{1/3}/D^{1/3}
, the comoving wavenumber associated with the mean PBH interspacing reads

\displaystyle k_{\rm PBH}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2\pi^{3}}{45}\right)^{1/3}\left(\frac{g_{\star,s}(T_{0})}{h_{\star}(T_{f})}\right)^{1/3}\left(\frac{\beta_{f}T_{f}}{D\,M_{\rm{PBH}}}\right)^{1/3}T_{0}

(270)

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{3.6\times 10^{16}~{\rm Mpc^{-1}}}{D^{1/3}}\frac{\gamma_{H}^{1/6}}{h^{1/3}_{\star}(T_{f})}\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}(T_{f})}\right)^{1/12}\left(\frac{\beta_{f}}{10^{-8}}\right)^{1/3}\left(\frac{10^{8}~\rm g}{M_{\rm PBH}}\right)^{1/2},

(271)

where we have set
a(T_{0})=1
,
T_{0}\simeq 2.73~\rm K
, and
g_{\star,s}(T_{0})\simeq 3.94
. For
D>1
, using Eqs. (
263
) and (
264
),

\displaystyle k_{\rm{PBH}}(D>1)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2\pi^{3}}{45}\right)^{1/3}\left(\frac{g_{\star,s}(T_{0})}{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}\right)^{1/3}\left(\frac{T_{\rm eva}}{M_{\rm{PBH}}}\right)^{1/3}T_{\rm 0}

(272)

\displaystyle\simeq\frac{4.9\times 10^{14}~{\rm Mpc^{-1}}}{h_{\star}^{1/3}(T_{\rm eva})}\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}\right)^{1/12}\left(\frac{g_{H\star}}{108}\right)^{1/6}\left(\frac{10^{8}~\rm g}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{5/6}.

(273)

The universe temperature
T_{H}
when the mode
k_{H}
enters the horizon,
aH=k_{H}
, is

T_{H}=5.8\times 10^{12}~{\rm GeV}~h_{\star}(T_{H})^{1/3}D^{1/3}\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}(T_{H})}\right)^{1/6}\left(\frac{k_{H}}{10^{20}~\rm Mpc^{-1}}\right),

(274)

where
D\equiv S(T_{0})/S(T_{H})
is the today to early-time comoving entropy density ratio with
S\propto g_{\star,s}T^{3}a^{3}
.
20
20

20

We have also used
g_{\star}(T_{0})\equiv 2+(7/8)\cdot 2\cdot N_{\rm eff}\cdot(4/11)^{4/3}\simeq 3.38
, and
g_{\star,s}(T_{0})\equiv 2+(7/8)\cdot 2\cdot N_{\rm eff}\cdot(4/11)\simeq 3.94
which assume
N_{\rm eff}\simeq 3.043

[
82
]
.

Plugging Eqs. (
249
) and (
257
) into Eq. (
274
), and dividing by Eq. (
270
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\rm eva}}{k_{\rm{PBH}}}~=~\left(\frac{3A}{8\pi}\right)^{\!1/3}\!\left(\frac{M_{\rm pl}}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{\!2/3}\!\left(\frac{h_{\star}(T_{f})}{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}\,\frac{D\,T_{\rm eva}}{\beta_{f}T_{f}}\right)^{\!1/3}

(275)

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\rm eq}}{k_{\rm{PBH}}}~=~\sqrt{2}\,c_{2}^{-2/3}\left(\frac{3A}{8\pi}\right)^{\!1/3}\!\left(\frac{M_{\rm pl}}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{\!2/3}\!D^{2/3},

(276)

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\rm f}}{k_{\rm{PBH}}}~=~\left(\frac{\gamma_{\rm H}}{\beta_{\rm c}}\frac{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\right)^{\!1/3}\!D^{-1/3},

(277)

with
c_{2}\equiv\left({g_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}/{g_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\right)^{1/4}\left({h_{\star}(T_{\rm eva})}/{h_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})}\right)
. Equations (
275
) and (
277
) are exact identities, since
k_{\rm eva}
and
k_{f}
cross the horizon deep in matter and radiation domination: the bracket in Eq. (
275
) equals unity by Eqs. (
263
) and (
260
), and Eq. (
277
) collapses to
(\gamma_{\rm H}/\beta_{f})^{1/3}
. The factor
\sqrt{2}
in Eq. (
276
) is that of Eq. (
267
), reflecting that
k_{\rm eq}
crosses at matter-radiation equality. Using Eq. (
264
) for
D
, these become

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\rm eva}}{k_{\rm{PBH}}}(D>1)~=~\left(\frac{3A}{8\pi}\right)^{1/3}\!\left(\frac{M_{\rm pl}}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{\!2/3}~\simeq~8.5\times 10^{-10}\left(\frac{g_{\rm H\star}}{108}\right)^{\!1/3}\left(\frac{10^{8}~\rm g}{M_{{\rm PBH},f}}\right)^{\!2/3},

(278)

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\rm eq}}{k_{\rm{PBH}}}(D>1)~=~\sqrt{2}\,c_{1}\gamma_{\rm H}^{1/3}\beta_{f}^{2/3}~\simeq~6.5\times 10^{-6}\,c_{1}\,\gamma_{\rm H}^{1/3}\left(\frac{\beta_{f}}{10^{-8}}\right)^{\!2/3},

(279)

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\rm f}}{k_{\rm{PBH}}}(D>1)~=~\left(\frac{\gamma_{\rm H}}{\beta_{f}}\right)^{1/3}~\simeq~4.6\times 10^{2}~\gamma_{\rm H}^{1/3}\left(\frac{10^{-8}}{\beta_{f}}\right)^{1/3},

(280)

where
c_{1}\equiv\left(g_{\star}(T_{\rm eq})h_{\star}^{4}(T_{\rm eq})/g_{\star}(T_{f})/h_{\star}^{4}(T_{f})\right)^{1/6}
. Finally, inserting Eq. (
268
) into Eq. (
265
) and into the entropy relation Eq. (
263
), the dilution factor and the number of e-foldings of the PBH-dominated era read

D\simeq 2^{-3/4}c_{2}\left(\frac{k_{\rm eq}}{k_{\rm eva}}\right)^{\!3/2},\qquad N_{\rm MD}\equiv\ln\!\left(\frac{a(T_{\rm eva})}{a(T_{\rm eq})}\right)=\frac{4}{3}\ln\!\left(c_{2}^{-1}D\right)\simeq 2\ln\!\left(\frac{k_{\rm eq}}{k_{\rm eva}}\right)-\ln 2\,,

(281)

with
2^{-3/4}\simeq 0.60
, the constant
2^{-3/4}
being the cube-root-free form of the
\sqrt{2}
in Eq. (
267
).

Appendix B
PBH mass distribution following Choptuik’s law

This appendix derives the PBH mass distribution used in Sec.
2.2
. We first specify the formation prescription and recall Choptuik scaling. We then introduce the compaction scale and the geometric factor
\kappa
, derive the mass distribution in the Press–Schechter and peak-theory approaches, summarize the characteristic mass scales in Tab.
5
, and finally discuss the narrow-peak limit.

B.1
PBH formation theory

We consider PBHs formed during radiation domination from the collapse of large superhorizon density perturbations. After horizon re-entry, a perturbation collapses if the comoving-gauge density contrast smoothed over a horizon volume,
\delta_{m}
, exceeds a critical threshold
\delta_{c}

[
69
]
. The fraction of the total energy density that collapses into PBHs of mass
M_{\rm{PBH}}
at the epoch when the horizon mass is
M_{H}
can be written as
[
303
,
58
,
171
,
227
]

\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},M_{H})}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}}=\int_{\delta_{\ell,c}}^{\infty}d\delta_{\ell}\,\frac{M_{\rm{PBH}}}{M_{H}}\,P(\delta_{\ell},M_{H})\,\delta_{D}\!\left[\ln\frac{M_{\rm{PBH}}}{M(\delta_{m}(\delta_{\ell}))}\right].

(282)

Here
P(\delta_{\ell},M_{H})
is the probability distribution of the linear density contrast smoothed on the Hubble scale at fixed
M_{H}
, and
\delta_{\ell,c}
is the value corresponding to the nonlinear threshold
\delta_{c}
. During radiation domination, the nonlinear and linear density contrasts are related by
[
93
,
341
]

\delta_{m}=\delta_{\ell}-\frac{3}{8}\delta_{\ell}^{2}.

(283)

The branch relevant for PBH formation is therefore

\delta_{\ell}=\frac{4}{3}\left(1-\sqrt{\Lambda}\right),\qquad\Lambda\equiv 1-\frac{3}{2}\delta_{m}.

(284)

Close to the collapse threshold, the PBH mass is governed by critical collapse. For a one-parameter family of initial data labeled by an amplitude
p
, there is a critical value
p_{c}
separating dispersion from black-hole formation. For
p>p_{c}
and sufficiently close to threshold, the solution first approaches a universal self-similar critical solution. Since this critical solution has only one unstable mode, the final black-hole mass is controlled by the distance to threshold and follows the Choptuik scaling law
[
81
,
4
,
132
,
267
,
285
,
245
,
177
,
287
,
288
,
340
,
173
,
208
]

M(\delta_{m})=\mathcal{K}\,M_{H}\,(\delta_{m}-\delta_{c})^{\gamma_{\rm M}}.

(285)

The coefficient
\mathcal{K}
depends on the perturbation profile and is typically of order a few, with
\mathcal{K}\simeq 4
often used as a reference value
[
287
,
288
]
. The exponent
\gamma_{\rm M}
is universal for a fixed matter content, and for a radiation fluid one finds
\gamma_{\rm M}\simeq 0.36

[
287
,
288
,
340
]
. The threshold
\delta_{c}
is profile dependent, with numerical studies giving the range
0.40\lesssim\delta_{c}\lesssim 0.67

[
284
,
126
]
. The scaling in Eq. (
285
) assumes approximate spherical symmetry, near-threshold collapse, and a fixed equation of state during the collapse.
The horizon mass at temperature
T_{H}
is

M_{H}(T_{H})=\frac{4\pi\rho}{3H^{3}}=0.95\times 10^{6}~{\rm g}\left(\frac{106.75}{g_{\star}(T_{H})}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{10^{13}~{\rm GeV}}{T_{H}}\right)^{2},

(286)

where
10^{7}~{\rm g}=5\times 10^{-27}~M_{\odot}
and
g_{\star}
is the number of relativistic degrees of freedom.
Substituting Eq. (
285
) into Eq. (
282
) and using
\delta_{D}(g(x))=\sum_{i}\delta_{D}(x-x_{i})/|g^{\prime}(x_{i})|
gives

\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},M_{H})}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}}=\frac{2\mathcal{K}}{\gamma_{\rm M}}\,\frac{q^{1+1/\gamma_{\rm M}}}{\sqrt{\Lambda}}\,P\!\left(\delta_{\ell}(M_{\rm{PBH}}),M_{H}\right),

(287)

with

q\equiv\frac{M_{\rm{PBH}}}{\mathcal{K}\,M_{H}},\qquad\delta_{m}(M_{\rm{PBH}})=\delta_{c}+q^{1/\gamma_{\rm M}},\qquad\Lambda=1-\frac{3}{2}\left(\delta_{c}+q^{1/\gamma_{\rm M}}\right).

(288)

The condition that the linear density contrast remains on the physical branch of Eq. (
284
) implies

M_{H}\geq M_{H}^{\rm min}(M_{\rm{PBH}})=\frac{M_{\rm{PBH}}}{\mathcal{K}\,(2/3-\delta_{c})^{\gamma_{\rm M}}}.

(289)

Equivalently, at fixed
M_{H}
the PBH mass satisfies

M_{\rm{PBH}}\leq M_{\rm{PBH}}^{\rm max}=\mathcal{K}(2/3-\delta_{c})^{\gamma_{\rm M}}M_{H}.

(290)

The remaining ingredient is the probability density
P(\delta_{\ell},M_{H})
, which we model using either Press–Schechter or peak theory.

B.2
From the peak scale
k_{f}
to the collapse scale
r_{m}

The Fourier scale
k_{f}
at which the curvature power spectrum peaks is not, in general, the real-space scale that controls collapse. The relevant scale is defined by the compaction function, which measures the excess mass inside a spherical region normalized by its areal radius
[
318
,
186
,
283
,
284
,
341
,
128
,
129
,
282
]

\mathcal{C}(r,t)\equiv 2\,\frac{\delta M(r,t)}{R_{\rm ar}(r,t)}=2\,\frac{M_{\rm MS}(r,t)-M_{b}(r,t)}{R_{\rm ar}(r,t)}.

(291)

Here
R_{\rm ar}
is the areal radius,
M_{\rm MS}
is the Misner–Sharp mass, and
M_{b}=4\pi\rho_{b}R_{\rm ar}^{3}/3
is the background FRW mass enclosed in the same areal radius. The characteristic radius of the overdensity is the radius
r_{m}
at which the compaction function is maximal,

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}(r_{m})=0.

(292)

For a curvature power spectrum peaked at
k_{f}
, we define the geometric factor

\kappa\equiv k_{f}r_{m}.

(293)

For the log-normal spectrum of Eq. (
307
),
\kappa
depends only on the width
\Delta
and is well fitted by
[
282
,
165
]

\kappa(\Delta)\simeq 1.55+\frac{1.19}{1+\left(\Delta/0.45\right)^{2.06}},\qquad 0\leq\Delta\leq 3.

(294)

This fit reproduces the tabulated values with a maximum absolute error below
5\times 10^{-3}
. Representative values are given in Tab.
5
. Since

M_{f}\equiv M_{H}(\mathcal{H}=k_{f}),\qquad M_{H}\propto\mathcal{H}^{-2}\quad\text{(radiation domination)},

(295)

the horizon mass associated with the compaction scale is

M_{H}(r_{m})\equiv M_{H}(\mathcal{H}=1/r_{m})=M_{H}(\mathcal{H}=k_{f}/\kappa)=\kappa^{2}M_{f}.

(296)

Thus the same physical distribution is shifted by a factor
\kappa^{2}
depending on whether masses are normalized to
M_{f}
or to
M_{H}(r_{m})
. With this convention, the critical-collapse relation becomes

M_{\rm{PBH}}=\mathcal{K}\,\kappa^{2}M_{f}\,(\delta_{m}-\delta_{c})^{\gamma_{\rm M}}.

(297)

B.3
PBH mass distribution

The probability density
P(\delta_{\ell},M_{H})
entering Eq. (
282
) can be modeled in two standard ways, the Press–Schechter formalism and peak theory, which differ in how collapsing regions are counted but share the same Choptuik induced infrared slope. Combining either prescription with the critical-collapse relation of Eq. (
297
) and integrating over horizon masses yields the formation-time mass distribution
\psi_{f}(M_{\rm{PBH}})
used throughout this work.

Press–Schechter formalism

In the Press–Schechter approach, the smoothed linear density contrast is evaluated at a random spatial point and modeled as a Gaussian field
[
303
,
58
]
,

P(\delta_{\ell},M_{H})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}\,\sigma_{0}(r_{H})}\exp\!\left[-\frac{\delta_{\ell}^{2}}{2\sigma_{0}^{2}(r_{H})}\right],

(298)

where
r_{H}=k_{H}^{-1}
is the comoving Hubble radius when the horizon mass is
M_{H}
. The spectral moments of the smoothed density contrast are

\sigma_{j}^{2}(r_{H})=\frac{16}{81}\int\frac{dk}{k}\,(kr_{H})^{4+2j}\tilde{W}^{2}(k,r_{H})\,T^{2}(k,r_{H})\,\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{R}}(k).

(299)

Here
\tilde{W}(k,R)=3j_{1}(kR)/(kR)
is a real-space top-hat window function
[
342
]
, and
T(k,R)=3j_{1}(kR/\sqrt{3})/(kR/\sqrt{3})
is the radiation-era transfer function in comoving gauge
[
223
]
.

21
21

21

Peak theory is often written in terms of the BBKS moments
[
39
]
, defined by
\sigma_{j,\rm BBKS}^{2}(R)=\int d^{3}k/(2\pi)^{3}\,k^{2j}P_{\delta}(k)W^{2}(kR)
when peaks of the density field are counted. Our moments are written in terms of the dimensionless combination
kr_{H}
and are related by
\sigma_{j}^{2}(r_{H})=r_{H}^{2j}\sigma_{j,\rm BBKS}^{2}(R=r_{H})
. If peaks of
\mathcal{R}
are counted instead,
P_{\delta}
should be replaced by
P_{\mathcal{R}}

[
135
]
.

Peak theory

Peak theory counts local maxima of the smoothed density contrast rather than random spatial points. Let
\delta_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})
be the density contrast smoothed on the Hubble scale
r_{H}
, and define its Hessian as

H_{ij}(\mathbf{x})\equiv\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\delta_{\ell}(\mathbf{x}).

(300)

The differential number density of peaks of height
\nu=\delta_{\ell}/\sigma_{0}
is
[
39
]

n_{\rm pk}(\nu)\equiv\left\langle\delta_{D}^{(3)}\!\big(\nabla\delta_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})\big)\,|\det H(\mathbf{x})|\,\Theta_{\rm pk}\!\big(H(\mathbf{x})\big)\,\delta_{D}\!\left(\nu-\frac{\delta_{\ell}(\mathbf{x})}{\sigma_{0}}\right)\right\rangle,

(301)

where
\Theta_{\rm pk}(H)=\prod_{a=1}^{3}\Theta(-\lambda_{a})
, and
\lambda_{a}
are the eigenvalues of
H_{ij}
. In the high-peak limit
\nu\gg 1
, the number density of peaks with height in
[\delta_{\ell},\delta_{\ell}+d\delta_{\ell}]
is
[
39
]

n_{\rm pk}(\delta_{\ell},M_{H})\,d\delta_{\ell}\simeq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}r_{H}^{3}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sqrt{3}\,\sigma_{0}}\right)^{3}\left(\frac{\delta_{\ell}}{\sigma_{0}}\right)^{3}\exp\!\left[-\frac{\delta_{\ell}^{2}}{2\sigma_{0}^{2}}\right]\frac{d\delta_{\ell}}{\sigma_{0}}.

(302)

To use this result in Eq. (
282
), the Press–Schechter probability density is replaced by the expected number of peaks inside one Hubble volume
V_{H}=4\pi r_{H}^{3}/3
,

P(\delta_{\ell},M_{H})\simeq\frac{1}{3\pi}\left(\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sqrt{3}\,\sigma_{0}}\right)^{3}\left(\frac{\delta_{\ell}}{\sigma_{0}}\right)^{3}\exp\!\left[-\frac{\delta_{\ell}^{2}}{2\sigma_{0}^{2}}\right].

(303)

The main effect of peak theory is the extra high-peak weight proportional to
\nu^{3}
. It enhances rare overdensities and gives a heavier high-mass tail than Press–Schechter, while the low-mass scaling induced by Choptuik critical collapse remains unchanged.

Mass distribution

The differential energy fraction at temperature
T
is obtained by integrating over horizon masses,

\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},T)}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}}\equiv\int d\ln M_{H}\,\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},M_{H})}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}}\,\frac{\rho(T_{H})}{\rho(T)}\,\frac{s(T)}{s(T_{H})}.

(304)

At the reference temperature
T_{f}
, defined by
k_{f}=\mathcal{H}
, we write

\beta_{f}\equiv\int d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}\,\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},T_{f})}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}},\qquad\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},T_{f})}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}}\simeq\int d\ln M_{H}\,\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},M_{H})}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}}.

(305)

The last expression neglects the small redshifting correction across the narrow range of horizon masses that dominates the integral. The normalized mass distribution at formation is

\psi_{f}(M_{\rm{PBH}})\equiv\frac{1}{\beta_{f}}\frac{d\beta(M_{\rm{PBH}},T_{f})}{d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}}.

(306)

In this work we use the log-normal curvature spectrum

\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{R}}(k)=\frac{\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{R}}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\,\Delta}\exp\!\left[-\frac{\ln^{2}(k/k_{f})}{2\Delta^{2}}\right],

(307)

where
k_{f}
is the peak scale,
\Delta
is the width, and
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{R}}
is the amplitude. The resulting distributions are shown in Fig.
6
. Their infrared behavior is universal,

\psi_{f}(M_{\rm{PBH}})\propto M_{\rm{PBH}}^{1+1/\gamma_{\rm M}},\qquad 1+\frac{1}{\gamma_{\rm M}}\simeq 3.78,

(308)

while the ultraviolet tail depends on the abundance prescription and on the shape of the curvature spectrum. Peak theory enhances rare peaks relative to Press–Schechter and therefore extends the distribution toward larger PBH masses, but it does not modify the universal low-mass power law.
The mean PBH mass is defined as the total PBH energy density divided by the total PBH number density. Since
\psi_{f}
is normalized as an energy fraction, one has

\langle M_{\rm{PBH}}\rangle=\left[\int d\ln M_{\rm{PBH}}\,\frac{\psi_{f}(M_{\rm{PBH}})}{M_{\rm{PBH}}}\right]^{-1}.

(309)

We denote the mean mass in units of
M_{f}
by

\gamma_{\rm H}^{(k_{f})}\equiv\frac{\langle M_{\rm{PBH}}\rangle}{M_{f}},

(310)

and the same mean mass in units of the horizon mass at the compaction scale by

\gamma_{\rm H}^{(r_{m})}\equiv\frac{\langle M_{\rm{PBH}}\rangle}{M_{H}(r_{m})}=\frac{\gamma_{\rm H}^{(k_{f})}}{\kappa^{2}}.

(311)

The numerical values used in this work are collected in Tab.
5
.
